     9→# Edit Module
    10→
    11→**Goal:** Modify existing BMAD module briefs or module structures while maintaining coherence and compliance.
    12→
    13→**Your Role:** You are the **Module Architect** — a specialist in BMAD module design and maintenance. You help users modify their modules while preserving integrity and functionality.
    14→
    15→---
    16→
    17→## WORKFLOW ARCHITECTURE
    18→
    19→This uses **step-file architecture** for disciplined execution.
    20→
    21→### Core Principles
    22→
    23→- **Micro-file Design**: Each step is a self contained instruction file
    24→- **Just-In-Time Loading**: Only the current step file is in memory
    25→- **Sequential Enforcement**: Sequence within the step files must be completed in order
    26→- **State Tracking**: Document progress in output file frontmatter
    27→- **Append-Only Building**: Build documents by appending content as directed
    28→
    29→### Step Processing Rules
    30→
    31→1. **READ COMPLETELY**: Always read the entire step file before taking any action
    32→2. **FOLLOW SEQUENCE**: Execute all numbered sections in order
    33→3. **WAIT FOR INPUT**: If a menu is presented, halt and wait for user selection
    34→4. **CHECK CONTINUATION**: If the step has a menu with Continue, only proceed when user selects 'C'
    35→5. **SAVE STATE**: Update frontmatter before loading next step
    36→6. **LOAD NEXT**: When directed, read fully and follow the next step file
    37→
    38→### Critical Rules
    39→
    40→- 🛑 **NEVER** load multiple step files simultaneously
    41→- 📖 **ALWAYS** read entire step file before execution
    42→- 🚫 **NEVER** skip steps or optimize the sequence
    43→- 💾 **ALWAYS** update frontmatter when writing final output for a step
    44→- 🎯 **ALWAYS** follow exact instructions in step files
    45→- ⏸️ **ALWAYS** halt at menus and wait for input
    46→- 📋 **NEVER** create mental todo lists from future steps
    47→- ✅ YOU MUST ALWAYS SPEAK OUTPUT In your Agent communication style with config `{communication_language}`
    48→
    49→---
    50→
    51→## INITIALIZATION SEQUENCE
    52→
    53→### 1. Configuration Loading

    55→Load and read full config from `{project-root}/_bmad/bmb/config.yaml` and resolve:

     1→---
     2→title: AST bridge naming mismatch causes silent T1-low degradation
     3→severity: high
     4→workflow: create-stack-skill
     5→steps: step-04-parallel-extract
     6→detected: 2026-03-28
     7→---
     8→
     9→# AST bridge naming mismatch causes silent T1-low degradation
    10→
    11→## Problem
    12→
    13→The workflow references `ast_bridge` as the tool for structural extraction at Forge/Forge+/Deep tiers, but this name does not resolve to any actual tool available in the execution environment. The real MCP tool is `mcp__ast-grep__find_code` (and related ast-grep tools like `find_code_by_rule`, `dump_syntax_tree`).
    14→
    15→When subagents are spawned for parallel extraction (step-04), they cannot resolve "ast_bridge" to an actionable tool. Instead of failing explicitly, they silently degrade to source reading (T1-low). The parent workflow receives T1-low results and proceeds without warning — the user sees "Deep tier" in the banner but gets Quick-tier confidence on every library.
    16→
    17→## Impact
    18→
    19→- **All 25 libraries** extracted at T1-low despite `forge-tier.yaml` confirming `ast_grep: true` and tier: Deep
    20→- The entire confidence tier system is undermined — Deep tier delivered the same results as Quick tier
    21→- The user has no indication that degradation occurred unless they inspect the evidence report
    22→- This is the highest-impact issue: it makes Forge/Forge+/Deep tiers functionally identical to Quick
    23→
    24→## Reproduction
    25→
    26→1. Run `[SF] Setup Forge` — detects ast-grep, sets tier to Forge or higher
    27→2. Run `[SS] Stack Skill` — step-04 spawns extraction agents
    28→3. Agents attempt to use "ast_bridge" — tool not found
    29→4. All extractions silently degrade to T1-low
    30→5. Final report shows Deep tier but 100% T1-low confidence
    31→
    32→## Proposed Fix
    33→
    34→### Option A: Tool name mapping table (recommended)
    35→
    36→Add a tool resolution table to `forge-tier.yaml` or a new `tool-mappings.yaml` in the sidecar:
    37→
    38→```yaml
    39→tool_mappings:
    40→  ast_bridge:
    41→    claude_code: "mcp__ast-grep__find_code"
    42→    cursor: "ast-grep"  # or whatever Cursor uses
    43→    cli_fallback: "sg run"
    44→  ccc_bridge:
    45→    claude_code: "/ccc"
    46→    cli_fallback: "ccc search"
    47→  qmd_bridge:
    48→    claude_code: "mcp__plugin_qmd-plugin_qmd__search"
    49→    cli_fallback: "qmd search"
    50→```
    51→
    52→Step-04 loads this mapping and resolves bridge names to actual tool invocations.
    53→
    54→### Option B: Direct tool names in workflow steps
    55→
    56→Replace all `ast_bridge` references in step files with the actual tool name (MCP or CLI), parameterized by IDE from `config.yaml`:
    57→
    58→```markdown
    59→**If ide is claude-code:** Use `mcp__ast-grep__find_code` with pattern `from $LIB import $$$`
    60→**If ide is cursor:** Use ast-grep via terminal `sg run ...`
    61→```
    62→
    63→### Option C: Explicit AST extraction prompt for subagents
    64→
    65→Include in step-04 a concrete extraction prompt template that subagents receive, which names the actual tool:
    66→
    67→```
    68→Use the ast-grep MCP tool (mcp__ast-grep__find_code) to find all import statements
    69→matching pattern: `from {library} import $$$` in language: python
    70→```
    71→
    72→## Files to modify
    73→
    74→- `workflows/skillforge/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-04-parallel-extract.md` — replace `ast_bridge` references
    75→- `workflows/skillforge/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-05-detect-integrations.md` — same for integration detection
    76→- Consider: `_memory/forger-sidecar/forge-tier.yaml` schema — add tool_mappings section
    77→- Consider: all workflow step files that reference bridge names (`ast_bridge`, `ccc_bridge`, `qmd_bridge`, `gh_bridge`)
    78→
```

     2→title: Manifest glob returns node_modules noise — missing exclusion patterns
     3→severity: medium

     6→detected: 2026-03-28
     7→---
     8→
     9→# Manifest glob returns node_modules noise — missing exclusion patterns
    10→
    11→## Problem
    12→
    13→Step-02 instructs: "Scan the project root (depth 0-1) for manifest files." The supporting `data/manifest-patterns.md` lists manifest filenames (package.json, pyproject.toml, etc.) but provides no exclusion rules for common artifact directories.
    14→
    15→When globbing for `package.json` at depth 0-1, the search returns 80+ results from `node_modules/`, `.venv/`, and other dependency directories. This pollutes the manifest list and forces manual filtering in the execution thread.
    16→
    17→## Impact
    18→
    19→- Glob for `package.json` returned 80+ node_modules results on the cognee project
    20→- Wasted context window on irrelevant results
    21→- Required manual re-scoping to root-level manifests only
    22→- On larger monorepos this could return thousands of false positives
    23→
    24→## Reproduction
    25→
    26→1. Run `[SS] Stack Skill` on any project with a `node_modules/` or `.venv/` directory
    27→2. Step-02 globs for manifest patterns
    28→3. Results include dependency-internal manifests
    29→
    30→## Proposed Fix
    31→
    32→Add an exclusion list to `data/manifest-patterns.md`:
    33→
    34→```markdown
    35→## Exclusion Patterns
    36→
    37→When scanning for manifests, ALWAYS exclude these directories:
    38→- `node_modules/`
    39→- `.venv/` / `venv/` / `.env/`
    40→- `__pycache__/`
    41→- `dist/` / `build/` / `target/`
    42→- `.git/`
    43→- `.next/` / `.nuxt/`
    44→- `vendor/` (PHP/Go)
    45→- Any directory starting with `.` (hidden directories)
    46→```
    47→
    48→And update step-02 instructions to reference these exclusions:
    49→
    50→```markdown
    51→Scan the project root (depth 0-1) for manifest files, **excluding directories listed in manifest-patterns.md exclusion section**.
    52→```
    53→
    54→Alternatively, the step could specify explicit glob patterns with exclusions built in:
    55→```
    56→{package.json,pyproject.toml,Cargo.toml,...} at depth 0-1, excluding **/node_modules/**
    57→```
    58→
    59→## Files to modify
    60→
    61→- `workflows/skillforge/create-stack-skill/data/manifest-patterns.md` — add exclusion section
    62→- `workflows/skillforge/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-02-detect-manifests.md` — reference exclusions in scan instructions
    63→
```

     7→---
     8→
     9→# CCC bridge invocation undefined — semantic augmentation unreachable
    10→
    11→## Problem
    12→
    13→Step-05 references CCC semantic augmentation for Forge+ and Deep tiers:
    14→
    15→> For each library pair that has exactly 1 co-import file (below the 2-file threshold), run `ccc_bridge.search("{libA} {libB}", source_root, top_k=10)` to find files where the two libraries interact semantically.
    16→
    17→The `ccc_bridge.search()` invocation does not correspond to any actual tool or command. The CCC system is invoked via:
    18→- Claude Code: `/ccc` skill (which triggers the `ccc` skill definition)
    19→- CLI: `ccc search <query>` command
    20→- The actual search interface and return format differ from what the workflow expects
    21→
    22→The workflow assumes `ccc_bridge` returns `{pair, files[]}` structured data, but the real CCC tool returns search results in a different format (markdown with file paths and code snippets).
    23→
    24→## Impact
    25→
    26→- CCC augmentation was completely skipped despite `ccc: true` and `ccc_daemon: "healthy"` in forge-tier
    27→- Library pairs at exactly 1 co-import file (below threshold) that might have qualified with semantic evidence were excluded
    28→- Forge+ tier's differentiating feature ("AST structural + CCC semantic co-import augmentation") is non-functional
    29→- On the cognee project, pairs like `pydantic + aiohttp` (1 file) and `fastapi + typing_extensions` (1 file) might have crossed the 2-file threshold with CCC augmentation
    30→
    31→## Reproduction
    32→
    33→1. Run `[SF] Setup Forge` — CCC detected, daemon healthy
    34→2. Run `[SS] Stack Skill` — step-05 attempts CCC augmentation
    35→3. `ccc_bridge.search()` cannot be resolved to any tool
    36→4. CCC augmentation silently skipped per "CCC failures: skip augmentation silently"
    37→
    38→## Proposed Fix
    39→
    40→Replace the abstract `ccc_bridge.search()` reference with concrete invocation instructions per IDE:
    41→
    42→```markdown
    43→**CCC Semantic Augmentation (Forge+ and Deep with ccc):**
    44→
    45→For each library pair at exactly 1 co-import file:
    46→
    47→**Claude Code:** Use the ccc skill to search: invoke `/ccc` with query `{libA} {libB} integration`
    48→  - Parse returned file paths from search results
    49→  - If CCC returns files where both libraries appear, add to co-import candidate list
    50→
    51→**CLI fallback:** Run `ccc search "{libA} {libB}" --top-k 10 --format paths`
    52→  - Parse stdout for file paths
    53→  - Filter to files containing imports of both libraries
    54→
    55→**Return format:** Extract file paths from CCC results, then verify each file
    56→actually imports both libraries (grep confirmation) before counting as co-import.
    57→```
    58→
    59→This connects to Issue #001 (tool name mapping) — a centralized tool resolution table would solve both issues.
    60→
    61→## Files to modify
    62→
    63→- `workflows/skillforge/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-05-detect-integrations.md` — replace `ccc_bridge.search()` with concrete invocations
    64→- Consider: shared `data/tool-invocation-guide.md` referenced by all steps that use tool bridges
    65→
```

     8→
     9→# QMD temporal enrichment has no implementation path — T2 confidence unreachable
    10→
    11→## Problem
    12→
    13→The Deep tier extraction strategy in step-04 promises:
    14→
    15→> **Deep Tier (adds to Forge):**
    16→> - Use qmd_bridge for temporal usage evolution
    17→> - Identify deprecated patterns and migration paths
    18→> - Track API changes across project history
    19→> - Confidence: T2 (QMD-enriched temporal context)
    20→
    21→This is entirely aspirational — no workflow step provides:
    22→1. What QMD queries to run
    23→2. What QMD collections to target (the `qmd_collections` array in forge-tier.yaml is empty)
    24→3. How to format QMD results into the extraction structure
    25→4. How to integrate temporal data with the per-library extraction
    26→5. What constitutes "temporal usage evolution" in practical terms
    27→
    28→The `qmd_bridge` reference has the same naming problem as `ast_bridge` (Issue #001), but even with correct tool naming, there's no implementation logic.
    29→
    30→## Impact
    31→
    32→- T2 confidence tier is unreachable — the confidence_distribution will always show `t2: 0`
    33→- Deep tier's differentiating value proposition ("Full intelligence — structural + contextual + temporal") is only partially delivered (contextual via CCC, if #003 is fixed)
    34→- The forge tier system advertises 4 tiers but functionally only Quick and Forge (with AST) produce different results
    35→- Users who achieve Deep tier have no additional capability over Forge+
    36→
    37→## Reproduction
    38→
    39→1. Run `[SF] Setup Forge` — QMD detected, tier set to Deep
    40→2. Run `[SS] Stack Skill` — step-04 extraction
    41→3. No QMD queries are ever issued
    42→4. Final confidence distribution shows 0 T2 entries
    43→
    44→## Proposed Fix
    45→
    46→### Phase 1: Define what temporal enrichment means for stack skills
    47→
    48→Add a new data file `data/temporal-enrichment-guide.md` that specifies:
    49→
    50→```markdown
    51→# Temporal Enrichment Guide
    52→
    53→## What QMD provides for stack skills
    54→
    55→QMD collections can contain:
    56→- Git commit history indexed as markdown (changelog, commit messages)
    57→- Documentation snapshots over time
    58→- API migration guides
    59→
    60→## Queries per library
    61→
    62→For each library in confirmed_dependencies:
    63→1. Search for deprecation notices: `{library} deprecated OR removed OR breaking change`
    64→2. Search for migration patterns: `{library} migration OR upgrade OR v{major}`
    65→3. Search for version-specific issues: `{library} version issue OR bug OR workaround`
    66→
    67→## Integration with extraction
    68→
    69→Temporal findings are appended to per_library_extractions[].temporal:
    70→- deprecated_exports: exports found in code but flagged as deprecated in temporal sources
    71→- migration_notes: known migration paths for the current version
    72→- version_warnings: version-specific gotchas
    73→
    74→## Confidence upgrade rule
    75→
    76→A library extraction upgrades from T1 to T2 ONLY when:
    77→- Base extraction is T1 (AST-verified) AND
    78→- At least one temporal finding exists with source citation
    79→```
    80→
    81→### Phase 2: QMD collection seeding
    82→
    83→The setup-forge workflow needs a step that:
    84→1. Checks if relevant QMD collections exist (changelog, docs)
    85→2. If not, offers to create and populate them from git history or doc files
    86→3. Registers created collections in `forge-tier.yaml` `qmd_collections[]`
    87→
    88→### Phase 3: Step-04 QMD query execution
    89→
    90→Add a concrete QMD query section to step-04 that runs after AST extraction:
    91→
    92→```markdown
    93→**If qmd_collections is non-empty AND tier is Deep:**
    94→For each library, run QMD searches per temporal-enrichment-guide.md.
    95→Merge temporal findings into per_library_extractions[].
    96→Upgrade confidence from T1 to T2 where temporal evidence exists.
    97→```

     1→---

     3→severity: low

     5→steps: step-03-rank-and-confirm, step-04-parallel-extract, step-05-detect-integrations
     6→detected: 2026-03-28
     7→---
     8→
     9→# Subprocess execution model unmapped to actual IDE tooling
    10→
    11→## Problem
    12→
    13→Steps 03, 04, and 05 all reference a "subprocess" execution pattern for parallelization:
    14→
    15→- Step-03: "**Launch a subprocess** that runs grep across all source files"
    16→- Step-04: "**Launch subprocesses in parallel** (max_parallel_generation) — one per confirmed library"
    17→- Step-05: "**Launch a subprocess** that greps across all source files to find files importing BOTH library A and library B"
    18→
    19→The workflow assumes a subprocess spawning capability that returns structured results, but does not map this to any actual execution mechanism in the target environments:
    20→
    21→- **Claude Code:** The Agent tool spawns parallel subagents — but the workflow doesn't mention this
    22→- **Cursor:** Cursor's agent model is different and may not support parallel subagents
    23→- **CLI/manual:** No subprocess model exists
    24→
    25→Each step includes a fallback: "If subprocess unavailable, perform in main thread sequentially." In practice, the executor must independently discover that "subprocess" maps to the Agent tool, guess the prompt format, and handle result aggregation — all of which the workflow could specify.
    26→
    27→## Impact
    28→
    29→- Execution quality depends on the operator discovering the Agent tool mapping independently
    30→- Sequential fallback negates the parallel design — step-04 with 25 libraries takes 5-10x longer sequentially
    31→- No guidance on how to structure subagent prompts for optimal results (what to include, what to exclude, return format)
    32→- `max_parallel_generation` referenced but never defined — how many parallel agents are safe?
    33→
    34→## Reproduction
    35→
    36→1. Run `[SS] Stack Skill` — reach step-04
    37→2. Workflow says "launch subprocesses in parallel"
    38→3. Executor must figure out: use Agent tool? How many? What prompt? What return format?
    39→4. If executor doesn't know about Agent tool, falls back to sequential (much slower)
    40→
    41→## Proposed Fix
    42→
    43→Add an execution model mapping section to the workflow header or a shared data file:
    44→
    45→```markdown
    46→## Execution Model: Subprocess Mapping
    47→
    48→When this workflow references "launch a subprocess", use the following
    49→mapping based on your IDE:
    50→
    51→### Claude Code
    52→Use the **Agent tool** to spawn parallel subagents:
    53→- Set `run_in_background: true` for parallel execution
    54→- Max parallel agents: 4-6 (to avoid context pressure)
    55→- Each agent receives a self-contained prompt with all needed context
    56→- Agent results are returned as text — parse structured data from output
    57→
    58→**Prompt template for extraction subagent:**
    59→> You are extracting library usage data from {project} at {path}.
    60→> RESEARCH ONLY — do not edit files.
    61→> For each library in [{list}], find imports, extract exports, document patterns.
    62→> Return structured report per library.
    63→
    64→### Cursor
    65→Use Cursor's built-in agent capabilities or sequential execution.
    66→Cursor agents share context differently — provide file paths, not content.
    67→
    68→### CLI / Manual
    69→Execute sequentially in main thread. Use background shell processes
    70→if available (`command &`).
    71→```
    72→
    73→Also define `max_parallel_generation`:
    74→
    75→```yaml
    76→# In config.yaml or workflow header
    77→max_parallel_generation: 5  # Max simultaneous subagent spawns
    78→```
    79→
    80→## Files to modify
    81→
    82→- `workflows/skillforge/create-stack-skill/workflow.md` — add execution model mapping section
    83→- `workflows/skillforge/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-04-parallel-extract.md` — reference mapping, specify agent prompt template
    84→- `workflows/skillforge/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-05-detect-integrations.md` — same
    85→- `workflows/skillforge/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-03-rank-and-confirm.md` — same
    86→- Consider: shared `data/execution-model.md` reusable across all workflows
    87→- Consider: define `max_parallel_generation` in `config.yaml` schema
    88→
```

### 2. CONFIDENCE TIER DEFINITIONS

**From `/src/knowledge/confidence-tiers.md` (lines 21-49):**

| Tier | Label | Source | Extraction Method | Available At | Citation Format |
|------|-------|--------|-------------------|--------------|-----------------|
| T1 | AST-verified | Source code | ast-grep structural parsing | Forge, Forge+, Deep | `[AST:filepath:Lnn]` |
| T1-low | Source-read | Source code | Pattern matching, file reading | Quick, Forge, Forge+, Deep | `[SRC:filepath:Lnn]` |
| T2 | QMD-enriched | Knowledge base | QMD semantic search + synthesis | **Deep Only** | `[QMD:collection:document]` |
| T3 | External reference | Docs, URLs | External documentation lookup | All tiers | `[EXT:url-or-reference]` |

**T2 Sub-types (lines 30-39):**
- **T2-past**: Closed issues, merged PRs, changelogs (surfaces in `references/` folder)
- **T2-future**: Open PRs, deprecation warnings, RFCs (surfaces in `SKILL.md` Section 4b + `references/`)

---

### 3. TIER CALCULATION RULES

**From `/src/workflows/setup-forge/data/tier-rules.md` (lines 14-33):**

```yaml
Priority 1 (highest): Deep    = ast-grep + gh + qmd (all three required)
Priority 2:           Forge+  = ast-grep + ccc
Priority 3:           Forge   = ast-grep (alone)
Priority 4 (default): Quick   = None (no tools)
```

**Edge cases (lines 28-33):**
- `gh` available but no `ast-grep` → Quick (gh alone doesn't unlock)
- `ast-grep + gh` but no `qmd` → Forge (qmd required for Deep)
- `ast-grep + qmd` but no `gh` → Forge (gh required for Deep)
- `ccc` available but no `ast-grep` → Quick (ccc alone doesn't unlock)

**Tool verification commands:**
- `ast-grep --version` (must return version, not just existence)
- `gh --version`
- `qmd status` (must indicate initialized/operational)
- `ccc --help` (binary) + `ccc doctor` (daemon health)

---

### 4. QMD COLLECTION REGISTRY ARCHITECTURE

**From `/src/knowledge/qmd-registry.md` (lines 1-150):**

**Registry Location:** `/src/forger/forge-tier.yaml` → `qmd_collections[]` array

**Collection Types:**

| Type | Producer | Content | Primary Consumers |
|------|----------|---------|-------------------|
| `extraction` | create-skill step-07 | Compiled SKILL.md, references, context-snippet | audit-skill (drift), update-skill (T2 enrichment) |
| `brief` | brief-skill step-05 | skill-brief.yaml — intent, scope, metadata | Portfolio-level search |
| `temporal` | create-skill step-03b | GitHub issues, PRs, releases, changelogs (T2) | step-04 enrichment (temporal annotations) |
| `docs` | create-skill step-03c | Fetched external documentation (T3) | step-04 enrichment (doc cross-reference) |

**Collection Lifecycle (lines 56-69):**
```
brief-skill writes brief → indexes {name}-brief → registers in forge-tier.yaml
    ↓
create-skill fetches temporal → indexes {name}-temporal → registers (Deep only)
    ↓
create-skill fetches docs → indexes {name}-docs → registers (Deep only)
    ↓
create-skill compiles skill → indexes {name}-extraction → registers
    ↓
audit-skill reads registry → queries {name}-extraction for drift baseline
update-skill reads registry → queries {name}-extraction for T2 enrichment
    ↓
setup-forge reads registry + QMD state → cleans orphans, removes stale entries
```

**Registry Schema Example (lines 31-43):**
```yaml
qmd_collections:
  - name: "my-lib-extraction"
    type: "extraction"
    source_workflow: "create-skill"
    skill_name: "my-lib"
    created_at: "2026-03-14"
  - name: "my-lib-temporal"
    type: "temporal"
    source_workflow: "create-skill"
    skill_name: "my-lib"
    created_at: "2026-03-14"
```

### 5. T2 TEMPORAL DATA SEEDING MECHANISMS

**Create-Skill Temporal Fetching (step-03b):**
- **Location:** `/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03b-fetch-temporal.md`
- **Lines:** 1-239
- **Tier Gate:** Deep tier only (lines 59)
- **Source:** GitHub repositories only (lines 60)
- **Requirements:** `gh` CLI (verified at line 61)

**Fetched Content Types (section 3, lines 78-150):**
1. **Issues** (last 100): `gh issue list` → `{staging}/issues.md`

3. **Releases** (last 10): `gh release list` → `{staging}/releases.md`

**Staging & Indexing (section 4, lines 152-192):**
- Write to: `_bmad-output/{skill-name}-temporal/`

**Error Handling (lines 184-188):**
- QMD indexing failure: warns but doesn't block workflow
- Registry update failure: warns but continues
- Cleanup failure: warns and continues
- **Master rule:** Temporal enrichment is best-effort; failures degrade gracefully

### SUMMARY OF POTENTIAL ISSUES

1. **T2 Data Availability Gate:** audit-skill and update-skill assume T2 data exists if at Deep tier, but gracefully skip if not found (no hard blocker)

2. **QMD Registry Stale Check:** If create-skill fails to update forge-tier.yaml after QMD indexing, audit-skill won't find the collection (but continues without semantic diff)

3. **Temporal Data Freshness:** step-03b caches temporal data for 7 days; if critical changes occur mid-week, they won't be reflected (documented behavior)

4. **Compose Mode vs. Temporal:** create-stack-skill in compose mode (lines 53-74 of step-04-parallel-extract.md) skips all extraction; compose-mode doesn't fetch temporal data (by design — it's synthesis-only)

5. **Deep Tier Dependency Chain:** Deep tier unlocks T2, but T2 is not required for skill generation — it enhances quality (audit semantic drift, update enrichment)

6. **Hidden Tier Downgrade:** If `qmd` becomes unavailable between setup-forge runs (uninstalled), tier drops to Forge; users must re-run setup-forge (documented in step-01-detect-and-tier.md lines 51-60)

### KEY ARCHITECTURAL FINDINGS

**1. Bridge Resolution Mechanism:**
- **Bridges are CONCEPTUAL INTERFACES only** - defined in `/src/knowledge/ccc-bridge.md:5` and `/src/workflows/create-skill/data/extraction-patterns.md:31`
- No code-based mapping exists; all resolution occurs via **TOOL/SUBPROCESS FALLBACK rule** documented in workflow files
- When a bridge is referenced, executors use:
  - **MCP tools** (preferred) if available in their environment
  - **CLI commands** as fallback
  - **Direct implementation** if neither available (graceful degradation)

**2. Bridge Implementation Specifications:**
- **ast_bridge** → `ast-grep` (MCP tool or CLI)
- **ccc_bridge** → `ccc` (MCP server tool or CLI `ccc search`/`ccc init`/`ccc index`/`ccc doctor`)
- **qmd_bridge** → `qmd` (MCP tool with `.search()` and `.vector_search()` methods)
- **gh_bridge** → GitHub API (no specific tool naming - implicit usage via agent capability)

**3. Tier-Dependent Tool Usage:**
- **Quick:** gh_bridge only (graceful if unavailable)
- **Forge:** ast_bridge (required for tier definition)
- **Forge+:** ast_bridge + ccc_bridge (ccc_bridge defines the tier)
- **Deep:** ast_bridge + qmd_bridge (qmd_bridge provides T2 enrichment)

**4. Files That WOULD Need Updating If Bridges Changed:**

**High Impact (Direct bridge calls):**
- `/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md`
- `/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-04-enrich.md` (5 qmd_bridge references)
- `/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-02b-ccc-discover.md`
- `/src/workflows/audit-skill/steps-c/step-02-re-index.md` (most complex)
- `/src/workflows/update-skill/steps-c/step-03-re-extract.md`
- `/src/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-05-detect-integrations.md`

**Medium Impact (Bridge usage patterns):**
- `/src/knowledge/ccc-bridge.md` (PRIMARY DOCUMENTATION - 8 references)
- `/src/knowledge/progressive-capability.md`
- `/src/workflows/create-skill/data/extraction-patterns.md` (PATTERN SPECS)
- All `step-01-init.md` files (tier mapping)

**Low Impact (References/documentation):**
- `/src/knowledge/qmd-registry.md`
- `/src/knowledge/overview.md`
- Various step files with single references

From `/src/workflows/create-skill/data/extraction-patterns.md:31`:

> **Note:** `ast_bridge.*`, `qmd_bridge.*`, and `ccc_bridge.*` references below are **conceptual interfaces**, not callable functions. They describe the operation to perform. Use ast-grep (MCP tool or CLI) for `ast_bridge.*` operations, QMD (MCP tool or CLI) for `qmd_bridge.*` operations, and ccc CLI or MCP for `ccc_bridge.*` operations. See the AST Extraction Protocol section below and the TOOL/SUBPROCESS FALLBACK rule for dispatch details.

This means **there is no single resolver to update** — the bridge names are intentionally abstract, and each workflow executor must resolve them based on available tools in their environment.

Here is the complete implementation plan for Issues 002 and 004.

---

## Implementation Plan: Issues 002 and 004

### Issue 002 -- Manifest Glob Missing Exclusions

#### Problem Summary

`manifest-patterns.md` (35 lines) lists manifest filenames and import patterns but has zero directory exclusion rules. `step-02-detect-manifests.md` line 102 says "Scan the project root (depth 0-1) for manifest files" with no exclusion clause. The result is that globbing for `package.json` returns 80+ hits from `node_modules/` and similar artifact directories.

Meanwhile, `step-03-rank-and-confirm.md` lines 94-97 already has exclusion patterns for import counting (test files, config files, build artifacts). These two sets of exclusions serve different purposes but share common directories. The fix must add exclusions to the manifest detection phase without creating duplication or inconsistency with step-03's existing patterns.

#### Observation: Step-03's Exclusions Already Include Some Overlap

Step-03 lines 94-97 exclude from import counting:
- Test files: `*/test/*`, `*_test.*`, `*.spec.*`, `*.test.*`
- Config files: `*.config.*`, `.eslintrc`, etc.
- Build artifacts: `dist/`, `build/`, `node_modules/`, `target/`, `__pycache__/`

The manifest scanning exclusions overlap partially (both need `node_modules/`, `dist/`, `build/`, `target/`, `__pycache__/`) but manifest scanning also needs ecosystem-specific vendored directories that are irrelevant to import counting.

#### Plan for manifest-patterns.md

Add a new `## Scan Exclusion Patterns` section after `## Detection Priority` (after line 22, before `## Filtering Rules`). This placement is logical because exclusions apply during the scan phase (Detection Priority), not during the filtering phase (Filtering Rules).

The new section should contain:

```markdown
## Scan Exclusion Patterns

When scanning for manifest files, ALWAYS exclude these directories from glob results:

### Dependency/Vendor Directories
- `node_modules/`
- `.venv/` / `venv/` / `.env/`
- `vendor/` (PHP Composer, Go modules)
- `Pods/` (iOS CocoaPods)

### Build Output Directories
- `dist/` / `build/` / `out/`
- `target/` (Rust, Java/Maven)
- `__pycache__/`
- `.next/` / `.nuxt/` / `.output/`

### Hidden and VCS Directories
- `.git/`
- Any directory starting with `.` (except project root hidden files like `.csproj`)

### Monorepo Note
For monorepo structures (e.g., `packages/*/package.json`), the depth 0-1 scan rule already limits scope. If monorepo manifest detection is needed at deeper levels, the above exclusions become critical to prevent scanning dependency trees within each package.
```

The "Monorepo Note" is important because the current depth 0-1 limit partially mitigates the problem, but users working with workspace configurations may want deeper scanning in the future, and this note preempts that.

#### Plan for step-02-detect-manifests.md

Two surgical changes:

**Change 1 -- Line 102 (Section 2 heading text):** Currently reads:
```
Scan the project root (depth 0-1) for manifest files:
```
Change to:
```
Scan the project root (depth 0-1) for manifest files, **excluding directories listed in the Scan Exclusion Patterns section of `{manifestPatterns}`**:
```

**Change 2 -- Add an exclusion reminder to the bullet list at lines 103-106.** After line 106 ("Note any unusual structures..."), add:
```
- **Apply exclusion patterns** from `{manifestPatterns}` — skip `node_modules/`, `.venv/`, `vendor/`, `dist/`, `build/`, `target/`, `.git/`, and all hidden directories when globbing
```

This is intentionally duplicative of the data file reference. The step instructions serve as prescriptive execution rules -- the agent needs the key exclusions inline so it does not need to context-switch to the data file during execution. The data file provides the exhaustive list and rationale.

#### Plan for manifest-patterns.md Import Counting section

Line 35 currently says: "Exclude test files, config files, and build artifacts from count" -- this is vague and inconsistent with step-03's explicit patterns. Expand line 35 to match step-03's precision:

```
- Exclude test files (`*/test/*`, `*_test.*`, `*.spec.*`, `*.test.*`), config files (`*.config.*`, `.eslintrc`, etc.), and build artifacts (`dist/`, `build/`, `node_modules/`, `target/`, `__pycache__/`) from count
```

This is a bonus consistency fix. The import counting exclusion in manifest-patterns.md should match step-03 exactly, since step-03 says "(Match patterns from manifest-patterns.md)" on line 88.

---

### Issue 004 -- QMD Temporal Enrichment No Implementation

#### Critical Nuance (Strategy Decision)

The issue file says "QMD temporal enrichment has no implementation path." My exploration reveals this is half-true:

1. **create-skill already has complete temporal fetching and enrichment** -- step-03b (239 lines) fetches GitHub issues/PRs/releases/changelogs and indexes them into QMD collections registered in `forge-tier.yaml`. step-04 (183 lines) then queries those collections to add T2 annotations to extracted functions.

2. **create-stack-skill's step-04 references T2 but has no queries.** Lines 108-111 describe what Deep tier should do but provide no concrete QMD queries, no collection discovery, no annotation format.

3. **In compose mode, step-04 skips extraction entirely** (line 55-73). T2 data is inherited from individual skills per the Confidence Tier Inheritance matrix in compose-mode-rules.md. This is already working as designed.

4. **In non-compose (code) mode, step-04 needs T2 implementation.** This is the actual gap.

**Strategy Decision: Reference, Do Not Replicate.**

Rationale:
- The QMD registry architecture (`src/knowledge/qmd-registry.md`) establishes a producer/consumer model. Producers are brief-skill and create-skill. Consumers are audit-skill and update-skill.
- create-stack-skill should be a **consumer**, not a duplicate producer. It should query existing temporal collections created by prior create-skill runs for individual libraries.
- If no temporal collections exist for a library (because create-skill was never run at Deep tier for it), create-stack-skill should degrade gracefully -- just as audit-skill and update-skill already do.
- This avoids duplicating the 239-line temporal fetching logic and avoids the question of "which repo do you fetch from" for multi-library stack skills (each library might come from a different GitHub repo).
- For libraries where create-skill has previously run at Deep tier, their `{library-name}-temporal` collections already exist in forge-tier.yaml's `qmd_collections` array and are queryable.

**What about libraries that have never been through create-skill?** In non-compose mode, create-stack-skill processes raw project dependencies detected from manifests -- these may never have had individual skills created. For these libraries, there is no existing temporal collection to reference. The correct behavior is: skip T2 enrichment for that library, label extraction as T1 or T1-low based on AST availability, and note in the extraction summary that T2 enrichment was unavailable. Optionally, suggest running create-skill for individual libraries first to enable Deep tier enrichment.

This is consistent with how update-skill step-03 lines 146-158 handles absent collections: "No QMD extraction collection found for {skill_name}. T2 enrichment skipped."

#### Plan for step-04-parallel-extract.md

Three changes to the non-compose mode path:

**Change 1 -- Replace the aspirational Deep Tier description (lines 107-111)** with concrete QMD query instructions:

Replace:
```
**Deep Tier (adds to Forge):**
- Use qmd_bridge for temporal usage evolution
- Identify deprecated patterns and migration paths
- Track API changes across project history
- Confidence: T2 (QMD-enriched temporal context)
```

With:
```
**Deep Tier (adds to Forge):**
- Perform all Forge tier extractions (T1)
- Additionally: query existing QMD temporal collections for each library
- Read the `qmd_collections` registry from `{sidecar_path}/forge-tier.yaml`
- For each library in `confirmed_dependencies`, search for a registry entry where `skill_name` matches the library name AND `type` is `"temporal"`
- **If a matching temporal collection exists:**
  - Query `qmd_bridge.search("{library_name} deprecated OR removed OR breaking change")` for deprecation context
  - Query `qmd_bridge.search("{library_name} migration OR upgrade")` for migration patterns
  - Query `qmd_bridge.search("{library_name} version issue OR bug OR workaround")` for version-specific warnings
  - Classify each result as T2-past (historical) or T2-future (planned changes) per confidence-tiers.md
  - Append temporal findings to the library's extraction as T2 annotations with `[QMD:{collection}:{doc}]` citations
- **If no matching temporal collection found:**
  - Log: "No temporal collection for {library_name}. T2 enrichment skipped."
  - Continue with T1/T1-low extraction only
- Confidence: T1 for structural (AST), T2 for temporal annotations (QMD-enriched)
```

**Change 2 -- Update the subprocess extraction structure (line 124-133)** to include temporal data fields.

Add to the extraction return structure after `warnings: []`:
```
  temporal: {
    deprecated_exports: ["export_name — reason [QMD:collection:doc]"],
    migration_notes: ["note [QMD:collection:doc]"],
    version_warnings: ["warning [QMD:collection:doc]"],
    t2_annotation_count: count
  }
```

**Change 3 -- Add a Deep tier note to the extraction summary (lines 154-166).**

After the confidence distribution line, add:
```
{If Deep tier:} **T2 enrichment:** {enriched_count}/{total_count} libraries had temporal collections available
{If libraries_without_temporal > 0:} **Tip:** Run [CS] Create Skill at Deep tier for individual libraries to generate temporal collections, then re-run [SS] for full T2 enrichment.
```

#### What About the "temporal-enrichment-guide.md" Data File?

The issue file proposes a new `data/temporal-enrichment-guide.md`. This is **not needed**. The concrete query instructions belong inline in step-04 (as specified above), following the pattern established by:
- create-skill's step-04-enrich.md lines 89-97, which specifies exact QMD queries inline
- update-skill's step-03-re-extract.md lines 142-160, which specifies collection discovery inline
- audit-skill's step-04-semantic-diff.md lines 84-88, which specifies collection discovery inline

All three existing consumers put their QMD query logic directly in the step file. A separate data file would break this convention and add indirection.

#### What About setup-forge QMD Collection Seeding?

The issue file proposes Phase 2: "setup-forge needs a step that checks if relevant QMD collections exist and offers to create them." This is **explicitly rejected** by the existing architecture. `setup-forge/steps-c/step-03-auto-index.md` line 35 states: "FORBIDDEN to create new QMD collections -- that responsibility belongs to create-skill." And `qmd-registry.md` lines 22-25 define the role split: setup-forge is a Janitor (cleanup only), not a Producer. Collection creation belongs to create-skill.

The correct approach is the one taken: graceful degradation when collections do not exist, with a user-facing tip to run create-skill first.

## Implementation Plan: Fix Issues 001, 003, and 005 -- Bridge Names and Subprocess Patterns

### 1. Architecture Decision: Hybrid (Centralized + Inline Expansion)

After thorough exploration, I recommend a **hybrid approach**: one centralized resolution reference file in `knowledge/`, plus targeted inline expansions in the 6 high-impact step files. Here is the reasoning:

**Why not purely centralized:**
- The codebase already uses the `knowledge/` folder for cross-cutting concepts and `data/` folders for workflow-specific operational details (see `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/knowledge/overview.md` lines 46-54 for the distinction).
- A centralized-only approach would require every step file to first load the resolution file before acting, but most step files already have 3-4 data file references in frontmatter. Adding a universal dependency creates a loading burden on steps that only reference one bridge type.
- The JIT loading protocol (overview.md lines 39-44) says to load only what is needed per task. A single resolution file covering all four bridges violates this principle for steps that use only one bridge.

**Why not purely inline:**
- There are 22+ files with bridge references. Inlining the full resolution table in all of them creates massive duplication.
- Bridge resolution mappings may change as tools evolve (new MCP servers, new CLI commands). A single source of truth for the mapping itself is essential.

**Hybrid design:**
1. **Create one centralized knowledge file** (`knowledge/tool-resolution.md`) that defines the canonical bridge-to-tool mappings and subprocess pattern definitions. This becomes the authoritative reference.
2. **Add it to the knowledge index** (`skf-knowledge-index.csv`) so it can be discovered via JIT loading.
3. **Expand the 6 high-impact step files** with compact inline resolution blocks that name the concrete tool to use (2-3 lines each, not the full mapping table). These blocks reference the knowledge file for the complete specification but are self-contained enough that an AI agent can execute the step without loading the knowledge file.
4. **Update `ccc-bridge.md`** to reference the new centralized file instead of being the sole location that explains the conceptual interface pattern.
5. **Expand the TOOL/SUBPROCESS FALLBACK rule** that already appears in ~55 step files (it is a universal rule) by adding a one-line clarification that points to `tool-resolution.md`.

This preserves backward compatibility: existing files still work because they already have the TOOL/SUBPROCESS FALLBACK rule. The change adds resolution clarity without removing any existing patterns.

### 2. Files to Create

**File 1: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/knowledge/tool-resolution.md`**

This is the core deliverable. It should contain:

**Section 1 -- Bridge Resolution Table:**
A mapping table with columns: Bridge Name | Conceptual Operation | Claude Code Resolution | Cursor Resolution | CLI Resolution | Fallback

Entries:
- `ast_bridge.scan_definitions()` -> `mcp__ast-grep__find_code` / `mcp__ast-grep__find_code_by_rule` (Claude Code MCP) | ast-grep MCP (Cursor) | `sg run` / `ast-grep -p` (CLI) | Source reading (T1-low)
- `ast_bridge.detect_co_imports()` -> `mcp__ast-grep__find_code_by_rule` with co-import YAML rule (Claude Code MCP) | ast-grep MCP (Cursor) | `ast-grep -p 'import $$$' --json=stream` (CLI) | grep-based co-import count
- `ccc_bridge.search()` -> `/ccc` skill (Claude Code) | ccc MCP server (Cursor) | `ccc search` (CLI) | Skip silently (standard Forge behavior)
- `ccc_bridge.ensure_index()` -> `/ccc` skill for indexing (Claude Code) | ccc MCP (Cursor) | `ccc init` + `ccc index` (CLI) | Skip silently
- `ccc_bridge.status()` -> `/ccc` skill status check (Claude Code) | ccc MCP (Cursor) | `ccc --help` + `ccc doctor` (CLI) | Not available = tools.ccc: false
- `qmd_bridge.search()` -> `mcp__plugin_qmd-plugin_qmd__search` (Claude Code MCP) | qmd MCP (Cursor) | `qmd search` (CLI) | Skip enrichment
- `qmd_bridge.vector_search()` -> `mcp__plugin_qmd-plugin_qmd__vector_search` (Claude Code MCP) | qmd MCP (Cursor) | `qmd vector_search` (CLI) | Use BM25 search results only
- `gh_bridge.list_tree()` -> `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/git/trees/{branch}?recursive=1` (all environments) | Also: `mcp__zread__get_repo_structure` (Claude Code MCP) | Fallback: direct file listing if local
- `gh_bridge.read_file()` -> `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/contents/{path}` (all environments) | Also: `mcp__zread__read_file` (Claude Code MCP) | Fallback: direct file read if local

**Section 2 -- Subprocess Pattern Definitions:**
Define what Pattern 1 through Pattern 4 actually mean in each IDE context:

- Pattern 1 (grep/search): Claude Code -> Grep tool or Bash with grep/rg | Cursor -> built-in search | CLI -> grep/rg
- Pattern 2 (per-file deep analysis): Claude Code -> Agent tool (sequential per file) | Cursor -> sequential analysis | CLI -> sequential script
- Pattern 3 (data operations): Claude Code -> main thread (Read + process) | Cursor -> main thread | CLI -> script
- Pattern 4 (parallel execution): Claude Code -> Agent tool with `run_in_background` or multiple parallel tool calls | Cursor -> parallel requests | CLI -> `xargs -P` or similar
- `max_parallel_generation`: Claude Code -> limited by Agent tool concurrency (typically 3-5 parallel calls) | Cursor -> IDE-dependent | CLI -> CPU core count or user-specified limit

**Section 3 -- Resolution Protocol:**
A step-by-step protocol for any step encountering a bridge or subprocess reference:
1. Check the bridge name against the resolution table
2. Check available tools in the current environment
3. Use the first available resolution in priority order: MCP tool -> CLI command -> Fallback
4. If no resolution available, apply the fallback behavior (which is always defined for each bridge)

**Section 4 -- Related Fragments:**
Links to `ccc-bridge.md`, `progressive-capability.md`, `confidence-tiers.md`

### 5. Potential Challenges

**Challenge 1: Inline resolution blocks adding verbosity.** Mitigation: keep inline blocks to 2-3 lines maximum, always pointing to `tool-resolution.md` for the full table. The format should be consistent: `**Tool resolution:** {concrete tool} (Claude Code), {concrete tool} (Cursor), or {concrete command} (CLI).`

**Challenge 2: The TOOL/SUBPROCESS FALLBACK universal rule.** This rule appears in 55+ step files. Rather than modifying all of them, the plan only modifies the 6 high-impact files where bridges appear as direct executable instructions. The universal rule continues to work as a backstop -- the new knowledge file gives it teeth.

**Challenge 3: Keeping bridge abstraction intact for multi-IDE support.** The plan preserves all `bridge_name.operation()` references. It does not rename them or replace them with concrete tool calls. Instead, it adds resolution blocks that say "this bridge resolves to X in environment Y." This maintains the abstraction while eliminating the ambiguity.

**Challenge 4: The `max_parallel_generation` undefined variable.** This appears exactly once (step-04-parallel-extract.md line 115) and is never defined. The plan defines it in `tool-resolution.md` and adds an inline definition at the usage site.

     1→# Manifest Detection Patterns
     2→
     3→## Supported Ecosystems
     4→
     5→| Ecosystem | Manifest File(s) | Dependency Key | Import Pattern |
     6→|-----------|-----------------|----------------|----------------|
     7→| JavaScript/TypeScript | package.json | dependencies, devDependencies | `import ... from '...'`, `require('...')` |
     8→| Python | requirements.txt, setup.py, pyproject.toml, Pipfile | install_requires, [project.dependencies] | `import ...`, `from ... import` |
     9→| Rust | Cargo.toml | [dependencies] | `use ...`, `extern crate` |
    10→| Go | go.mod | require | `import "..."` |
    11→| Java | pom.xml, build.gradle | dependencies | `import ...` |
    12→| Ruby | Gemfile | gem | `require '...'`, `require_relative` |
    13→| PHP | composer.json | require, require-dev | `use ...`, `require_once` |
    14→| .NET | *.csproj | PackageReference | `using ...` |
    15→
    16→## Detection Priority
    17→
    18→1. Search project root for manifest files (depth 0-1)
    19→2. Parse each found manifest to extract dependency names
    20→3. Normalize names across ecosystems (e.g., `@scope/package` → `package`)
    21→4. Deduplicate across multiple manifests
    22→
    23→## Filtering Rules
    24→
    25→- Exclude dev-only dependencies unless they appear in production imports
    26→- Exclude build tools (webpack, babel, eslint, etc.) unless significantly imported
    27→- Include all runtime dependencies by default
    28→- Flag transitive dependencies that appear in direct imports
    29→
    30→## Import Counting
    31→
    32→For each dependency, count distinct files that import it:
    33→- Use grep patterns from Import Pattern column
    34→- Count unique file paths, not total import statements
    35→- Exclude test files, config files, and build artifacts from count
    36→

    51→**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise.
    52→
    53→### 0. Check Compose Mode
    54→
    55→**If `compose_mode` is true:**
    56→
    57→"**Extraction data already available from individual skills. Skipping extraction phase.**"

Build a `per_library_extractions[]` entry for each skill with the following fields:

    62→- `usage_patterns`: usage patterns from the SKILL.md usage section

    64→

    74→

    76→
    77→### 1. Prepare Extraction Plan
    78→
    79→**AST Tool Availability Check (Forge/Deep only):**
    80→
    81→This workflow operates on local project files and installed library packages. Remote source resolution does not apply — libraries are analyzed as they exist within the project's dependency tree.
    82→
    83→**If AST tool is unavailable at Forge/Deep tier:**
    84→
    85→⚠️ **Warn the user explicitly:** "AST tools are unavailable — extraction will use source reading (T1-low). Run [SF] Setup Forge to detect and configure AST tools for T1 confidence."
    86→
    87→Degrade to Quick tier extraction. Note the degradation reason in context for the evidence report.
    88→
    89→**Per-file AST failure handling:**
    90→
    91→If ast-grep fails on an individual file (parse error, unsupported syntax), fall back to source reading for that file only. Label the affected file's results T1-low; unaffected files retain T1. Log a warning noting which file degraded and why.
    92→
    93→For each library in `confirmed_dependencies`, determine extraction strategy based on forge tier:
    94→
    95→**Quick Tier:**
    96→- Read source files that import the library
    97→- Extract usage patterns from import statements and function calls
    98→- Identify key exports used in this project
    99→- Confidence: T1-low (source reading inference)
   100→
   101→**Forge Tier (adds to Quick):**
   102→- Use ast_bridge to analyze structural exports from library source
   103→- Extract function signatures, type definitions, class hierarchies
   104→- Map parameter types and return types
   105→- Confidence: T1 (AST-verified structural extraction)
   106→
   107→**Deep Tier (adds to Forge):**
   108→- Use qmd_bridge for temporal usage evolution
   109→- Identify deprecated patterns and migration paths
   110→- Track API changes across project history
   111→- Confidence: T2 (QMD-enriched temporal context)
   112→
   113→### 2. Launch Parallel Extraction
   114→
   115→**Launch subprocesses in parallel** (max_parallel_generation) — one per confirmed library:
   116→
   117→Each subprocess:
   118→1. Reads all files importing the library (from step 03 file lists)
   119→2. Extracts key exports used in this project (functions, classes, types, constants)
   120→3. Identifies usage patterns (initialization, configuration, common call patterns)
   121→4. Labels confidence tier based on extraction method
   122→5. Returns structured extraction to parent:
   123→
   124→```
   125→{
   126→  library: "name",
   127→  version: "from_manifest",
   128→  exports_found: ["fn1", "fn2", "Type1"],
   129→  usage_patterns: ["pattern description with file:line"],
   130→  confidence: "T1|T1-low|T2",
   131→  files_analyzed: count,
   132→  warnings: []
   133→}
   134→```
   135→
   136→**If parallel subprocess unavailable:** Process libraries sequentially in main thread. Report progress after each library.
   137→
   138→### 3. Handle Extraction Failures
   139→
   140→For each library extraction:
   141→
   142→**Success:** Store extraction result.
   143→
   144→**Partial failure:** Store partial result with warnings, continue with other libraries.
   145→
   146→**Complete failure:** Log failure reason, exclude from stack skill, note in report.
   147→
   148→"**Warning:** Extraction failed for {library}: {reason}. Excluding from stack skill."
   149→
   150→**If ALL extractions fail:** HALT — cannot produce meaningful stack skill.
   151→
   152→### 4. Display Extraction Summary
   153→
   154→"**Library extraction complete.**
   155→
   156→| Library | Exports | Patterns | Confidence | Status |
   157→|---------|---------|----------|------------|--------|
   158→| {name} | {count} | {count} | {tier} | ✓ |
   159→| {name} | {count} | {count} | {tier} | ✓ |
   160→| {name} | — | — | — | ⚠ partial |
   161→
   162→**Results:** {success_count}/{total_count} libraries extracted
   163→**Confidence distribution:** T1: {count}, T1-low: {count}, T2: {count}
   164→{If warnings:} **Warnings:** {warning_count} issues noted
   165→
   166→**Proceeding to integration detection...**"
   167→
   168→### 5. Auto-Proceed to Next Step
   169→
   170→Load, read the full file and then execute `{nextStepFile}`.
   171→
   172→---
   173→
   174→## 🚨 SYSTEM SUCCESS/FAILURE METRICS
   175→
   176→### ✅ SUCCESS:
   177→
   178→- All confirmed libraries have extraction attempted
   179→- Extractions cite actual file:line references
   180→- Confidence tiers correctly assigned per extraction method
   181→- Partial failures handled gracefully (skip library, continue)
   182→- Extraction summary displayed with accurate counts
   183→- Auto-proceeded to step 05
   184→
   185→### ❌ SYSTEM FAILURE:
   186→
   187→- Fabricating exports not found in source code
   188→- Not assigning confidence tiers to extractions
   189→- Halting on single library failure (should degrade gracefully)
   190→- Analyzing cross-library integrations (step 05's job)
   191→- Returning raw file contents instead of structured extractions
   192→
   193→**Master Rule:** Extract per-library only. Every export must trace to actual code. Degrade gracefully on individual failures.
   194→

# Edit Module: Fix All 5 TODO Issues + Hidden Impacts

## Context

Five issues were identified in `_bmad-output/todo/` affecting the create-stack-skill, create-skill, audit-skill, update-skill, and analyze-source workflows. Issues 001, 003, and 005 share a root cause (abstract bridge/subprocess names don't resolve to concrete tools). Issue 002 is a missing exclusion pattern. Issue 004 is a partially-missing T2 temporal enrichment implementation.

**Key discovery:** Bridges are *intentionally* conceptual interfaces (documented in `extraction-patterns.md:31`), but the resolution note exists in only ONE file. All other files reference bridges without explaining how to resolve them, causing silent degradation.

**Key nuance on Issue 004:** create-skill already HAS full temporal fetching (step-03b, 239 lines). The gap is only in create-stack-skill's non-compose mode step-04.

---

- **Subprocess Pattern Definitions**: Pattern 1-4 mapped to Claude Code Agent tool, Cursor agents, CLI parallel execution

{
  "file_path": "/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/knowledge/tool-resolution.md",
  "content": "# Tool Resolution\n\nBridge names (`ast_bridge`, `ccc_bridge`, `qmd_bridge`, `gh_bridge`) and subprocess patterns (Pattern 1–4) used throughout SKF workflows are **conceptual interfaces**, not callable functions. This document provides the canonical mapping from abstract names to concrete tools per IDE environment.\n\n## Bridge Resolution Table\n\n| Bridge | Operation | Claude Code | Cursor | CLI | Fallback |\n|--------|-----------|-------------|--------|-----|----------|\n| `ast_bridge` | `scan_definitions()` | `mcp__ast-grep__find_code` or `mcp__ast-grep__find_code_by_rule` | ast-grep MCP | `sg run` / `ast-grep -p` | Source reading (T1-low) |\n| `ast_bridge` | `detect_co_imports()` | `mcp__ast-grep__find_code_by_rule` with co-import YAML rule | ast-grep MCP | `ast-grep -p 'import $$$' --json=stream` | grep-based co-import count |\n| `ccc_bridge` | `search(query, root, top_k)` | `/ccc` skill search | ccc MCP server | `ccc search \"{query}\" --path {root} --top {top_k}` | Skip silently |\n| `ccc_bridge` | `ensure_index(root)` | `/ccc` skill indexing | ccc MCP server | `ccc init` + `ccc index {root}` | Skip silently |\n| `ccc_bridge` | `status()` | `/ccc` skill status | ccc MCP server | `ccc --help` + `ccc doctor` | Unavailable = `tools.ccc: false` |\n| `qmd_bridge` | `search(query)` | `mcp__plugin_qmd-plugin_qmd__search` | qmd MCP server | `qmd search \"{query}\"` | Skip enrichment |\n| `qmd_bridge` | `vector_search(query)` | `mcp__plugin_qmd-plugin_qmd__vector_search` | qmd MCP server | `qmd vector_search \"{query}\"` | Use BM25 search only |\n| `gh_bridge` | `list_tree(owner, repo, branch)` | `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/git/trees/{branch}?recursive=1` | gh CLI | `gh api ...` | Direct file listing if local |\n| `gh_bridge` | `read_file(owner, repo, path)` | `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/contents/{path}` | gh CLI | `gh api ...` | Direct file read if local |\n\n## Subprocess Pattern Definitions\n\nWorkflow steps reference \"subprocess\" execution with numbered patterns. These map to concrete mechanisms per IDE:\n\n| Pattern | Purpose | Claude Code | Cursor | CLI |\n|---------|---------|-------------|--------|-----|\n| **Pattern 1** (grep/search) | Search across files for imports, co-imports | Grep tool or Bash with `rg` | Built-in search | `grep` / `rg` |\n| **Pattern 2** (per-file deep analysis) | Deep analysis of individual files or units | Agent tool (sequential per unit) | Sequential analysis | Per-unit script |\n| **Pattern 3** (data operations) | QMD queries, severity classification, data transforms | Main thread (Read + process) | Main thread | Script |\n| **Pattern 4** (parallel execution) | Parallel per-library extraction, parallel diffing | Agent tool with multiple parallel calls or `run_in_background: true` | Parallel requests (IDE-dependent) | `xargs -P` or background processes |\n\n### max_parallel_generation\n\nReferenced by Pattern 4 steps. Defines the maximum concurrent subprocess spawns:\n\n| Environment | Recommended Value | Notes |\n|-------------|-------------------|-------|\n| Claude Code | 3–5 | Concurrent Agent tool calls; higher values may cause context pressure |\n| Cursor | IDE-dependent | Follow Cursor's agent concurrency limits |\n| CLI | CPU core count or user-specified | Use `xargs -P {n}` or equivalent |\n\n## Resolution Protocol\n\nWhen a workflow step references a bridge name or subprocess pattern:\n\n1. **Identify** the bridge name (`ast_bridge`, `ccc_bridge`, etc.) or pattern number (Pattern 1–4)\n2. **Check tool availability** in `{sidecar_path}/forge-tier.yaml` → `tools.*` section\n3. **Resolve** using the tables above — use the first available option in priority order: MCP tool → CLI command → Fallback\n4. **Apply fallback** if no resolution is available — fallbacks are always defined and never block the workflow\n5. **Log degradation** when falling back — note the reason in context for the evidence report\n\n## Related Fragments\n\n- [ccc-bridge.md](ccc-bridge.md) — CCC semantic discovery interface and lifecycle\n- [progressive-capability.md](progressive-capability.md) — Quick/Forge/Forge+/Deep tier philosophy\n- [confidence-tiers.md](confidence-tiers.md) — T1/T1-low/T2/T3 trust model and citation formats\n- [qmd-registry.md](qmd-registry.md) — QMD collection producer/consumer/janitor architecture\n"
}

     1→# SKF Knowledge Overview
     2→
     3→## Principle
     4→
     5→Agent-level knowledge files capture cross-cutting principles and patterns that Ferris applies across multiple workflows. They are distinct from workflow `data/` files, which contain step-specific operational artifacts. Knowledge is loaded just-in-time when a workflow step directs — never preloaded into context.
     6→
     7→## Rationale
     8→
     9→The SKF module has 12 workflows containing numerous operational `data/` files embedded in step directories. These data files define schemas, templates, scoring formulas, and extraction rules tightly coupled to individual steps. But several principles — zero hallucination, confidence tiers, provenance — cut across multiple workflows and need a single authoritative source rather than fragmented repetition.
    10→
    11→Without a knowledge folder:
    12→- Cross-cutting principles get duplicated across workflow steps
    13→- Updates to foundational rules require changes in multiple locations
    14→- Ferris has no centralized reference for judgment calls that span workflows
    15→
    16→With knowledge files:
    17→- Each principle has one canonical definition
    18→- Workflow steps reference knowledge files instead of re-stating rules
    19→- Ferris loads only the knowledge relevant to the current task
    20→
    21→## Knowledge Map
    22→
    23→| File                                                       | Purpose                                                           | Key Workflows                      |
    24→|------------------------------------------------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------------|------------------------------------|
    25→| [zero-hallucination.md](zero-hallucination.md)             | Every claim traces to source; uncitable content excluded          | AN, BS, CS, QS, SS, US, AS, TS, RA |
    26→| [confidence-tiers.md](confidence-tiers.md)                 | T1/T1-low/T2/T3 trust model and citation formats                  | AN, BS, CS, QS, SS, US, AS, TS, EX, RA, VS |
    27→| [progressive-capability.md](progressive-capability.md)     | Quick/Forge/Forge+/Deep tier philosophy and behavior adaptation          | SF, AN, BS, CS, QS, SS, US, AS, TS, EX, VS, RA |
    28→| [agentskills-spec.md](agentskills-spec.md)                 | agentskills.io output format principles and compliance            | CS, QS, SS, US, TS, EX             |
    29→| [skill-lifecycle.md](skill-lifecycle.md)                   | End-to-end pipeline, artifact flow, workflow selection            | All 12                             |
    30→| [provenance-tracking.md](provenance-tracking.md)           | Provenance-map.json entries and file_entries, evidence reports, claim traceability | CS, QS, SS, US, AS, TS             |
    31→| [manual-section-integrity.md](manual-section-integrity.md) | \[MANUAL\] marker preservation and merge algorithm                | CS, US, AS, TS, EX                 |
    32→| [qmd-registry.md](qmd-registry.md)                         | Progressive QMD collection registry and collection gate principle | SF, BS, CS, US, AS                 |
    33→| [ccc-bridge.md](ccc-bridge.md)                             | ccc_bridge interface, indexing lifecycle, Forge+ discovery-before-extraction | SF, AN, CS, SS, US, AS             |
    34→| [architecture-verification.md](architecture-verification.md) | Pre-code verification pipeline, integration verdicts, iteration loop | VS, RA, SS                        |
    35→| [doc-fetcher.md](doc-fetcher.md)                           | T3 external documentation fetching, quarantine rules, tool-agnostic | CS, QS                             |
    36→| [split-body-strategy.md](split-body-strategy.md)           | Selective vs full split-body, detection pattern, agent accuracy   | CS, QS, US, TS, EX                |
    37→| [overview.md](overview.md)                                 | Knowledge map and JiT loading protocol (this file)               | All (entry point)                  |
    38→
    39→## JiT Loading Protocol
    40→
    41→1. Ferris receives a task or workflow activation
    42→2. Consult `skf-knowledge-index.csv` to identify relevant knowledge fragments by tags or description. The `tier` column classifies fragments as `core` (always relevant when the domain applies) or `extended` (load only when the specific scenario is active — e.g., `doc-fetcher.md` only when `doc_urls` are present in the brief)
    43→3. Load only the files needed for the current task — never bulk-load all knowledge
    44→4. Apply knowledge principles during workflow execution alongside step-specific `data/` files
    45→
    46→## Knowledge vs. Data
    47→
    48→| Aspect    | Knowledge Files (`knowledge/`)       | Data Files (`data/`)                    |
    49→|-----------|--------------------------------------|-----------------------------------------|
    50→| Scope     | Cross-cutting, multi-workflow        | Single workflow, single step            |
    51→| Content   | Principles, patterns, judgment rules | Schemas, templates, scoring formulas    |
    52→| Loading   | JiT via index lookup                 | JiT via step instruction                |
    53→| Examples  | Zero hallucination, confidence tiers | skill-brief-schema.md, scoring-rules.md |
    54→| Stability | Rarely changes                       | Changes with workflow evolution         |
    55→
    56→## Related Fragments
    57→
    58→- [confidence-tiers.md](confidence-tiers.md) — the trust model that underpins extraction and scoring
    59→- [skill-lifecycle.md](skill-lifecycle.md) — the end-to-end pipeline connecting all workflows
    60→- [progressive-capability.md](progressive-capability.md) — tier philosophy that affects every workflow
    61→
    62→_Source: synthesized from module.yaml, README.md, and workflow README_
    63→
```

    85→**Search query construction:**
    86→
    87→For each function, derive the **module context** from the extraction inventory's source file path (e.g., `src/graph/neo4j/index.ts` → module context `graph neo4j`). This context improves search relevance by scoping results to the function's subsystem without adding extra queries.
    88→
    89→**Primary searches (BM25 — always runs, no GPU/VRAM dependency):**
    90→
    91→1. **Issues/PRs:** `qmd_bridge.search("{module_context} {function_name}")` — find related discussions scoped by module context
    92→2. **Changelog entries:** `qmd_bridge.search("{function_name} changelog")` — find version history, breaking changes (kept generic — changelogs rarely use module paths)
    93→3. **Migration notes:** `qmd_bridge.search("{function_name} migration deprecated breaking")` — find deprecation or migration context using exact keyword matching
    94→
    95→**Supplemental search (best-effort — requires GPU/VRAM, may fail):**
    96→
    97→4. **Semantic migration context:** `qmd_bridge.vector_search("{module_context} {function_name} migration deprecated")` — adds semantic matches (synonyms, paraphrases) that BM25 keyword search may miss. Merge results with search #3, deduplicating by document ID. If `vector_search` fails (VRAM, GPU driver, model loading), discard silently — the BM25 results from search #3 provide baseline coverage.
    98→
    99→**For each QMD result:**
   100→
   101→- Create a T2 annotation: `[QMD:{collection}:{doc}]`
   102→- Classify temporal relevance:
   103→  - **T2-past:** Historical context (why it was built, what it replaced)
   104→  - **T2-future:** Forward-looking context (planned changes, deprecation warnings)
   105→
   106→**Handling QMD failures:**
   107→
   108→- If qmd_bridge is unavailable: skip all enrichment, note in context

    56→
    57→### 1. Determine Extraction Strategy
    58→
    59→Based on forge tier detected in Step 01:
    60→
    61→**Quick tier (no AST tools):**
    62→- Read source files via gh_bridge or direct file I/O
    63→- Extract export names by text pattern matching (function/class/type declarations)
    64→- Confidence label: T1-low
    65→
    66→**Forge tier (ast-grep available):**
    67→- Use ast_bridge to perform AST extraction per source file
    68→- Extract: export name, type (function/class/type/const), full signature, file path, line number
    69→- Confidence label: T1
    70→
    71→**Forge+ tier (ast-grep + ccc available):**
    72→- Identical extraction to Forge tier: use ast_bridge for AST extraction per source file
    73→- Confidence label: T1
    74→- CCC rename detection available (see section 4b)
    75→
    76→**Deep tier (ast-grep + QMD available):**

    79→- Confidence labels: T1 for structural, T2 for temporal context

    82→
    83→Identify all source files in the source directory that contain public exports.
    84→
    85→"**Scanning source files...**"

    87→- List all relevant source files (exclude test files, config files, build artifacts)
    88→- Count files to process
    89→- Report: "Found {count} source files to extract."
    90→
    91→### 3. Extract Current Exports
    92→
    93→**DO NOT BE LAZY — For EACH source file, launch a subprocess that:**
    94→1. Loads the source file
    95→2. Extracts all public exports using tier-appropriate method
    96→3. Records: export name, type, signature, file path, line number, confidence tier
    97→4. Returns structured findings to parent
    98→
    99→**If subprocess unavailable:** Perform extraction in main thread, processing each file sequentially.
   100→
   101→**Build extraction snapshot:**
   102→```
   103→{
   104→  "extraction_date": "{timestamp}",
   105→  "confidence_tier": "{tier}",
   106→  "source_root": "{source_path}",
   107→  "files_scanned": {count},
   108→  "exports": [
   109→    {
   110→      "name": "{export_name}",
   111→      "type": "function|class|type|const|interface",
   112→      "signature": "{full signature}",
   113→      "file": "{relative_path}",
   114→      "line": {line_number},
   115→      "confidence": "T1|T1-low|T2"

   117→  ]
   118→}

   120→
   121→### 4. Deep Tier Enhancement (Deep Only)
   122→
   123→**IF forge tier is Deep:**
   124→
   125→Read the `qmd_collections` registry from `{sidecar_path}/forge-tier.yaml`.
   126→
   127→Find the collection entry matching the current skill: look for an entry where `skill_name` matches the current skill being audited AND `type` is `"extraction"`.
   128→
   129→**If a matching extraction collection is found:**
   130→Query qmd_bridge against the `{skill_name}-extraction` collection for temporal context on each extracted export:
   131→- When was this export first added?
   132→- Has it been modified recently?
   133→- What is its usage frequency across the codebase?
   134→- How does the current extraction compare to the previously compiled skill content?
   135→
   136→Append temporal metadata to each export in the snapshot.
   137→
   138→**If no matching collection found in registry:**
   139→Log: "No QMD extraction collection found for {skill_name}. Temporal enrichment skipped. Re-run [CS] Create Skill to generate the collection."
   140→Continue without T2 enrichment — this is not an error.
   141→
   142→**IF forge tier is Quick, Forge, or Forge+:**
   143→Skip this section. Temporal context requires Deep tier.
   144→
   145→### 4b. CCC Rename Detection (Forge+ and Deep with ccc)
   146→
   147→**IF `tools.ccc` is true in forge-tier.yaml:**
   148→
   149→For each export in the skill baseline that was NOT found at its recorded file path during re-extraction (potential "deleted" export):
   150→
   151→1. Run `ccc_bridge.search("{export_name}", source_root, top_k=5)` to find candidate current locations
   152→2. If CCC returns files containing the export name:
   153→   - Run ast-grep verification on each candidate file
   154→   - If verified at a new location: reclassify from "deleted" to "moved" with the new file:line reference
   155→   - This reduces false-positive structural drift findings where exports were relocated, not removed
   156→3. If CCC returns no results or verification fails: keep the "deleted" classification

   158→CCC failures: skip rename detection silently, proceed with standard structural diff.

   146→Read the `qmd_collections` registry from `{sidecar_path}/forge-tier.yaml`.
   147→
   148→Find the collection entry matching the current skill: look for an entry where `skill_name` matches the skill being updated AND `type` is `"extraction"`.
   149→
   150→**If a matching extraction collection is found:**
   151→Launch a subprocess that loads qmd_bridge and for each changed export:
   152→1. Queries the `{skill_name}-extraction` collection for semantic context related to the export
   153→2. Searches for usage patterns, documentation references, temporal history
   154→3. Returns T2 evidence per export (usage frequency, context snippets, related concepts)
   155→
   156→**If no matching collection found in registry:**
   157→Log: "No QMD extraction collection found for {skill_name}. T2 enrichment skipped. Re-run [CS] Create Skill to generate the collection."
   158→Continue without T2 enrichment — extraction still produces T1 results.
   159→

   103→

If CCC returns additional files where both libraries appear, add them to the pair's co-import candidate list and re-evaluate against the 2-file threshold.
   107→
   108→For pairs that already qualify (2+ files), CCC is not needed for detection — but the CCC results may surface additional integration files for richer classification in section 3.
   109→
   110→CCC failures: skip augmentation silently, proceed with grep-only results.
   111→
   112→### 3. Classify Integration Types

     5→`ccc_bridge.*` references in workflow steps are **conceptual interfaces**, not callable functions. They describe a semantic code discovery operation to perform. Use the `ccc` MCP server tools (when available) or `ccc` CLI commands to execute these operations. See the TOOL/SUBPROCESS FALLBACK rule — if ccc is unavailable, the calling step falls back to direct ast-grep or source reading without ccc pre-discovery.
     6→
     7→## Rationale
     8→
     9→Without ccc pre-discovery, extraction steps scan all source files uniformly — processing them in directory order or entry-point-first order. On large codebases (500+ files), this means AST extraction in CLI streaming mode uses `head -N` cutoffs that may miss relevant exports in files that appear late in the scan. Integration detection in Stack Skill relies on grep-based co-import counting, which misses semantic relationships between libraries that don't appear in the same file.
    10→
    11→With ccc pre-discovery:
    12→- Extraction steps receive a relevance-ranked file queue — the most semantically important files are processed first, before any streaming cutoff
    13→- Integration detection gains semantic augmentation — pairs below the 2-file co-import threshold can be evaluated via natural language queries
    14→- Audit workflows can detect renamed/moved exports via semantic search before classifying them as deleted
    15→
```

    95→
    96→## Anti-Patterns
    97→
    98→- Using ccc_bridge results as citations without ast-grep verification — ccc output is never a provenance citation
    99→- Blocking a workflow because ccc is unavailable — ccc is always optional
   100→- Running ccc_bridge.ensure_index() without checking ccc_index.status first — unnecessary re-indexing
   101→- Passing ccc results directly to the extraction inventory — they are candidates, not extractions
   102→- Listing ccc as "unavailable" in reports for Quick/Forge tiers — ccc is a Forge+ capability, not something Quick/Forge tiers are missing
   103→

```
    78→
    79→**Context:** The create-skill extraction step adapts its strategy based on the forge tier.
    80→
    81→**Implementation:**
    82→- **Quick:** Reads source files, applies pattern matching for exports and signatures, produces T1-low citations. Uses `gh_bridge` for file structure when available as a convenience, not a requirement.
    83→- **Forge:** Uses `ast_bridge.scan_definitions()` for structural parsing, produces T1 citations with exact AST node types. Falls back to source reading for files ast-grep cannot parse.
    84→- **Forge+:** Identical extraction to Forge, plus ccc semantic pre-discovery narrows the file set before ast-grep runs. For large codebases, `ccc_bridge.search()` produces a ranked candidate list. ast-grep operates on that list rather than the full tree. All results remain T1 (AST-verified) — ccc is upstream, not in the extraction chain.
    85→- **Deep:** Identical extraction to Forge, plus QMD enrichment in a separate step. T2 annotations layer on top of the T1 base without replacing it.
    86→
    87→**Key Points:**
    88→- Extraction quality increases with tier, but every tier produces complete output
    89→- Forge and Deep share the same extraction core — Deep adds enrichment, not better parsing
    90→- Fallback within a tier (e.g., ast-grep fails on one file) degrades gracefully to T1-low for that file only
    91→
    92→### Example 4: Tier-Adaptive Scoring
```

    25→---
    26→
    27→## Forge Tier (AST Available)
    28→
    29→Structural extraction via ast-grep — verified exports with line-level citations.
    30→
    31→> **Note:** `ast_bridge.*`, `qmd_bridge.*`, and `ccc_bridge.*` references below are **conceptual interfaces**, not callable functions. They describe the operation to perform. Use ast-grep (MCP tool or CLI) for `ast_bridge.*` operations, QMD (MCP tool or CLI) for `qmd_bridge.*` operations, and ccc CLI or MCP for `ccc_bridge.*` operations. See the AST Extraction Protocol section below and the TOOL/SUBPROCESS FALLBACK rule for dispatch details.
    32→
    33→### Strategy
    34→
    35→1. Detect language from brief or file extensions
    36→2. Use ast-grep to extract all exports from `path` for the given `language` (scan definitions)
    37→3. For each export: function name, full signature, parameter types, return type, line number
    38→4. Use ast-grep to detect co-imported symbols in `path` for the given `libraries[]`
    39→5. Build extraction rules YAML for reproducibility
```

   320→
   321→## Tool Ecosystem
   322→
   323→### 7 Tools
   324→
   325→| Tool | Wraps | Purpose |
   326→|------|-------|---------|
   327→| **`gh_bridge`** | GitHub CLI (`gh`) | Source code access, issue mining, release tracking, PR intelligence |
   328→| **`skill-check`** | [thedaviddias/skill-check](https://github.com/thedaviddias/skill-check) | Validation + auto-fix (`check --fix`), quality scoring (0-100), security scan, split-body, diff comparison |
   329→| **`tessl`** | [tessl](https://tessl.io) | Content quality review, actionability scoring, progressive disclosure evaluation, AI judge with suggestions |
   330→| **`ast_bridge`** | ast-grep CLI | Structural extraction, custom AST queries, co-import detection |
   331→| **`ccc_bridge`** | cocoindex-code | Semantic code search, project indexing, file discovery pre-ranking |
   332→| **`qmd_bridge`** | QMD (local search) | BM25 keyword search, vector semantic search, collection indexing |
   333→| **`doc_fetcher`** | Environment web tools | Remote documentation fetching for T3-confidence content. Tool-agnostic — uses whatever web fetching is available (Firecrawl, WebFetch, curl, etc.). Output quarantined as T3. |
   334→
   335→### Conflict Resolution
   336→
   337→When tools disagree, higher priority wins for instructions. Lower priority is preserved as annotations:
   338→
   339→| Priority | Source | Tool |
   340→|----------|--------|------|
   341→| 1 (highest) | AST extraction | `ast_bridge` |
   342→| 1b | CCC discovery (pre-ranking) | `ccc_bridge` |
   343→| 2 | QMD evidence | `qmd_bridge` |
   344→| 3 | Source reading (non-AST) | `gh_bridge` |
   345→| 4 | External documentation | `doc_fetcher` |
   346→
   347→### Internal Utility
   348→
   349→**`manifest_reader`** detects and parses dependency files across ecosystems:
   350→
   351→- **Full support:** `package.json`, `pyproject.toml`, `requirements.txt`, `Cargo.toml`, `go.mod`
   352→- **Basic support:** `build.gradle`, `pom.xml`, `Gemfile`, `composer.json`
   353→
   354→---

    96→
    97→## Progressive Capability Model
    98→
    99→SKF uses an additive tier model. You never lose capability by adding a tool.
   100→
   101→| Tier | Required Tools | What You Get |
   102→|------|---------------|-------------|
   103→| **Quick** | None (`gh_bridge`, `skill-check`, `tessl` used when available) | Source reading + spec validation + content quality review. Best-effort skills in under a minute. **Note:** Quick Skill (QS) is tier-unaware by design — it always runs at community tier regardless of installed tools. |
   104→| **Forge** | + `ast_bridge` (ast-grep) | Structural truth. AST-verified signatures. Co-import detection. T1 confidence. |
   105→| **Forge+** | + `ccc_bridge` (cocoindex-code) | Semantic discovery. CCC pre-ranks files by meaning before AST extraction. Better coverage on large codebases. |
   106→| **Deep** | `ast_bridge` + `gh_bridge` (gh) + `qmd_bridge` (QMD). CCC optional — enhances when installed. | Knowledge search. Temporal provenance. Drift detection. Full intelligence. |
   107→
   108→Setup detects your installed tools and sets your tier automatically:
   109→
   110→```
```
